# Active context

Last updated: 2026-08-20

## GAG main executable reconstruction

- Reorganized CMake ownership around the source tree. The root now handles project-wide configuration and enters only its immediate `src/` and `tools/` children. The new `src/CMakeLists.txt` owns the GAG executable/tests and enters `src/xtet`; the new `tools/CMakeLists.txt` enters `tools/xtet_resource_extractor`; and the user-staged `xtet/` move is reflected by the XTET test include path. Recursive source globbing was removed completely. Each source-owning CMake file contributes an explicit manifest through its intermediate directory to the root formatting targets. All obsolete build-time XTET SFS configuration, fixed-path lookup, deployment, and payload-dependent test wiring were removed; runtime loading remains independent of CMake. The stale startup-test arguments now use the renamed `--xtet` option.

- Replaced XTET's deleted `tools/cdf_extractor/inflate.c` dependency with fetched zlib. `SfsArchive::decode_block` now decodes SFS encoding 8 as raw DEFLATE through `inflateInit2(..., -MAX_WBITS)`, preserves the recovered acceptance of a complete 32 KiB block even when the end marker cannot be consumed, and otherwise requires `Z_STREAM_END`. `xtet_core` links `ZLIB::ZLIBSTATIC`; the deleted source entries and language overrides are gone. The current Win32 Debug build succeeds, all 3 configured CTest tests pass, formatting passes, and no `cdf_extractor` or `inflate_data_bounded` references remain.

- Replaced the extractor's PE resource high-bit literals `0x80000000` and `0x7fffffff` with the structural expressions `(1u << 31)` and `~(1u << 31)`. The unsigned literal avoids signed left-shift issues while making the flag-bit operation explicit.

- Inlined the extractor's one-use PE32 magic, resource-directory index, and data-directory capacity constants at their use sites, removing the three namespace-level constants per the user's preference.

- Removed all explicit `std::streamoff` and `std::streamsize` casts from the extractor. File offsets and buffer sizes now pass directly to `seekg`/`read`; only the required byte-buffer-to-`char *` casts remain. MSVC accepts the standard stream conversions without warnings.

- Replaced the extractor's PE optional-header offset arithmetic with a complete PE32 `OptionalHeader` and `DataDirectory` representation. The extractor now validates the declared optional-header size, PE32 magic, and directory count; accesses the resource directory structurally; and reads section headers sequentially immediately afterward. PE32+ support was removed because the only supported input is the original 32-bit `XTETDLL.DLL`.

- Moved input-stream ownership into `extract_sfs(source)`, the sole consumer of the DLL. `extract()` now coordinates extraction and output only, while `extract_sfs()` opens, validates, reads, and closes its own `ifs`.

- Audited extractor stream positioning and removed the redundant initial `seekg(0)`: `extract_sfs()` receives the newly opened DLL stream already positioned at zero. Remaining seeks cross PE header regions or follow section/resource offsets and cannot safely be replaced by sequential reads.

- Applied the user's extractor control-flow convention: single-statement `if` bodies no longer use braces; braces remain on loops and multi-statement blocks.

- Removed redundant `{}` value-initialization from extractor scalars and PE structures that are immediately filled by stream reads. Stream-state checks now precede every use of newly read sizes and offsets, so malformed/truncated input cannot expose an uninitialized value.

- Removed the redundant packing pragmas around the extractor's PE structures. Their field layouts naturally have the required PE sizes under the supported Windows toolchains; no size assertions were added per the user's request.

- Applied the user's extractor formatting convention of two blank lines between top-level struct definitions and function definitions. The repository formatter already permits this spacing through `MaxEmptyLinesToKeep: 3`.

- Removed the remaining local lambdas from `extract_sfs()`. PE resource RVA mapping and each type/name/language directory read are now written directly at their point of use, making the single-purpose extractor a fully linear operation. The extractor builds, reproduces the expected 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`, and has no whitespace errors.

- Renamed the extractor's file-stream locals to `ifs` and `ofs`, following the user's preferred convention.

- Changed `xtet_resource_extractor::extract` path parameters from `std::string_view` to `const std::string &`. Both paths are passed directly to file-stream constructors and therefore require owned, null-terminated strings; this removes the redundant stream-opening copies and the now-unused `<string_view>` include. The extractor builds, reproduces the expected 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`, and formatting passes.

- Hid the extractor's PE parsing intermediate inside `extract_sfs(input)`. `extract()` now passes only the DLL stream and receives only the SFS payload; `extract_sfs` invokes `parse_pe` internally because no other caller consumes `PeImage`. The extractor builds, reproduces the 2,867,586-byte output, and formatting/whitespace checks pass.

- Converted every explicit cast in `xtet_resource_extractor` from C++ named-cast syntax to C-style casts, including stream offsets/sizes, PE integer widening, and byte-buffer character views. The scope is limited to this extractor. The named-cast scan is empty, the extractor builds and reproduces the 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`, and formatting/whitespace checks pass.

- Simplified extractor validation to direct stream behavior. The DLL is now opened without `ate`; `tellg`, total-file-size storage, numeric-size checks, and resource-directory range bookkeeping were removed. Each parser stage seeks to the PE-derived position, reads its required structure or payload, and immediately rejects a failed stream operation. Section RVA membership remains the only address translation needed. The rebuilt extractor produces `XTETDLL.SFS` with the established SHA-256; CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, the removed-plumbing scan is empty, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Fixed the user's one-expression extractor destination simplification: `path.stem().string() + ".SFS"` already produces `std::string`, so the trailing `.string()` was invalid. The corrected direct `extract(...)` call builds and produces the expected 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`; formatting and whitespace checks pass.

- Simplified extractor destination naming in `main()` with `std::filesystem::path`: the source path comes directly from `argv[1]`, and the current-directory destination is `source.stem() + ".SFS"`. This replaces manual slash, filename, extension, and empty-stem parsing while leaving stream-based extraction unchanged. The extractor builds and produces the expected 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`; formatting and whitespace checks pass.

- Reshaped the extractor entry flow to `extract(destination, source)`. `main()` now derives `<source-stem>.SFS` from the command-line DLL path and owns that naming policy before calling extraction. The standalone `write_file`, scalar `read_at`, templated `read_at`, and payload `read_bytes` helpers were removed; each PE/resource parser stage performs its own direct seek/read, while `extract()` directly writes the returned payload. The rebuilt tool produces `XTETDLL.SFS` with the established SHA-256, CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Changed `xtet_resource_extractor` from preloading the complete DLL to bounded random-access stream parsing. It opens the DLL with `ate` only to obtain its size, seeks directly to DOS/PE headers, section headers, resource directories, resource name, and data entry, and allocates a byte vector only when reading the final embedded SFS payload. Small vectors retain only section headers and the currently traversed resource-directory level. Running it against the original 3,151,360-byte DLL produced the same 2,867,586-byte archive with the same SHA-256 as the previous extractor. The C++23 extractor builds, CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Removed the extractor-local `Bytes` alias. `xtet_resource_extractor` now spells `std::vector<uint8_t>` explicitly in storage, parameters, and return types. The C++23 extractor builds, formatting passes, the alias scan is empty, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Removed `std::` qualification from all 5,646 uses of C-compatible scalar typedefs across first-party production code, tools, headers, and tests: fixed-width integers, `size_t`, `ptrdiff_t`, `intptr_t`, `uintptr_t`, and `uintmax_t`. Corresponding `<cstdint>`/`<cstddef>` includes were changed to `<stdint.h>`/`<stddef.h>` so the unqualified names are provided portably in the global namespace. C++-library-owned types such as `std::string`, `std::vector`, and `std::streamsize` remain qualified. The complete AMD64 C++23 Debug build succeeds, CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, the qualifier scan is empty, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Removed `std::filesystem::path` from `xtet_resource_extractor`. Its input, basename, extension, and output-name parsing now use `std::string_view`; owned strings are created only when opening a standard file stream or constructing the relative `<dll-stem>.SFS` output filename. Because the output name has no directory component, it is written to the current working directory without querying it. The C++23 extractor builds and successfully reproduces the 2,867,586-byte `XTETDLL.SFS`; CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Raised every first-party CMake target from C++17 to C++23: `gag`, fixes-on/fixes-off startup tests, resource tests, `xtet_core`, XTET SFS tests, `cdf_extractor`, and `xtet_resource_extractor`. The resource extractor now uses C++23 `std::println` for stdout/stderr diagnostics and no longer includes iostreams. The complete AMD64 Debug build succeeds, CTest passes 4/4, all target declarations report `cxx_std_23`, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Removed the runtime `XTETDLL.SFS` filename constant. The `/GAME` handler takes the final component of its script-supplied DLL path, validates the supported `XTETDLL.DLL` identity case-insensitively, replaces that component's `.DLL` extension with `.SFS`, and passes the derived filename through the internal linked-XTET initialization interface. XTET resolves that supplied basename beside `gag.exe`; its loading and validation errors also report the supplied filename. A regression test proves `C:\GAG\xtetdll.dll` initializes with `xtetdll.SFS`. AMD64 Debug builds, CTest 4/4, formatting, and whitespace checks pass.

- Simplified `xtet_resource_extractor` to `xtet_resource_extractor <original-dll>`. It reads the DLL without loading it, performs bounded PE/resource-directory traversal to locate the single named `XTETSFS` RCDATA payload, and writes only `<dll-stem>.SFS` in the current working directory. The `Reader` wrapper, output-directory argument, SHA-256 implementation, JSON metadata, RC file, temporary output directory, and BIN naming were removed. For `XTETDLL.DLL`, the output is `XTETDLL.SFS`; CMake now requires and deploys that filename. A run against the immutable original DLL produced 2,867,586 bytes identical to the prior payload. AMD64 Debug targets build, CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors.

- Added explicit pointer-cursor zones for the standalone `--gagboy` Start and Exit controls. The rectangles come from the parsed `XTETDLL.SFS` scene geometry: Start is `(149,49,88,84)` and Exit is `(478,426,66,54)`. Both zones select the existing `TET` hand cursor at priority 20; the earlier broad left/right control-bank zones did not cover either edge control. Tests assert both exact script definitions. The AMD64 Debug executable and targeted tests build, CTest passes 4/4, formatting passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors. Interactive hover confirmation remains.

- Kept the standalone `--gagboy` software cursor visible while XTET is paused. Original `XTETDLL.DLL` disassembly confirms `HandleGameMouseButton` pauses XTET and posts host message `0x7ffc/0x10`, then posts `0x7ffc/0x20` on resume; GAG uses these to suspend/resume the surrounding script plan. In the crafted standalone launch that plan owns the only visible software cursor, so suspending it made the Resume button cursorless even though XTET's own worker/audio were already paused. GAG now suppresses only those surrounding-plan flag transitions in gagboy startup mode while preserving the original messages and behavior for normal story invocation. Tests cover both normal forwarding and standalone suppression; AMD64 Debug builds, CTest 4/4, formatting, and whitespace checks pass. Interactive pause/resume cursor confirmation remains.

- Restored the standalone `--gagboy` software cursor over the visual controls. GAG intentionally hides the Win32 cursor and draws script-defined mouse resources; the crafted section had supplied the surround and `/GAME` event but omitted the `TET`/`TNM` mouse definitions, `Go` command, and priority zones present in `IVIEW.CIN::VGB1`. The in-memory section now reproduces the original high/low-color cursor assets, a hidden/neutral global zone, and the two higher-priority button rectangles `(180,185,45,200)` and `(505,90,45,200)`, so the hand pointer is selected over the clickable control banks while XTET continues receiving the actual mouse messages. AMD64 Debug builds and CTest pass 4/4; interactive cursor confirmation remains.

- Corrected the initial `--gagboy` black screen. The synthetic `GAGBOY.CFG` bytes were available through `LoadRuntimeResource`, but `ConstructRuntimeResource` first calls the independent type detector. Because the virtual filename is absent from the physical CDF, that detector returned generic type zero, so the resource was constructed as opaque data and its `[CFG]` tree was never activated. Gagboy mode now classifies the case-insensitive final path component `GAGBOY.CFG` as configuration type 4 before archive/file probing; the existing loader then supplies and caches the in-memory bytes through the normal configuration path. Both startup test variants cover the initial uncached, archive-backed lookup and CTest passes 4/4. Interactive `gag.exe --gagboy` confirmation remains.

- Statically integrated the reconstructed XTET minigame into `gag.exe`. CMake now builds an internal `xtet_core` static library and no longer defines or emits `XTETDLL.DLL` or `xtet_loader`; the obsolete export definition and loader source were removed. GAG's `/GAME` handler accepts only a case-insensitive, optionally path-qualified `XTETDLL.DLL` compatibility name, binds the linked init/window/execute callbacks directly, throws `std::invalid_argument` for every other name before changing runtime state, and performs explicit idempotent XTET shutdown on unload. XTET now owns bytes loaded from executable-adjacent `XTETDLL.SFS`, reports missing/empty/unreadable/malformed payloads clearly, and no longer uses an embedded RCDATA resource. `--gagboy` selects a cached in-memory `GAGBOY.CFG` that loads `VE-GBNEW.BMP` from the normal CDF, runs `/GAME:XTETDLL.DLL:GAGBoy::Score`, and then `/QUIT`; normal startup still selects `START.CFG`. The configured AMD64 Debug build, fixes-on/fixes-off startup tests, resource test, XTET SFS test, CTest 4/4, formatting, and whitespace checks pass. Interactive verification of normal story invocation and `gag.exe --gagboy` remains.

- Corrected the AMD64 Continue/autosave regression. The on-disk `AutoSave.cdf` was structurally valid but its sole `START.CFG` serialized the title menu (`MM::CONT_DIS=ON`) rather than gameplay. GAG's private `0x40000000` callback passes a `RuntimeTreeNode`; the original x86 code views its `name/parent/flags` prefix through a compact state-record overlay. The reconstruction retained that overlay after native pointers widened, so `ProcessStateActivation` read the wrong `parent` and `NOSAVE` flag offsets and allowed the title menu's `/MESSAGE::3010` to serialize over the retained gameplay snapshot. The callback and credits path now use canonical `RuntimeTreeNode` fields, and screenshot naming reads only the common leading name bytes. AMD64 Debug builds, fixes-on/fixes-off tests, CTest 4/4, and formatting pass. The already-corrupted autosave cannot recover lost state; interactive verification requires loading/starting gameplay, entering the in-game menu, exiting to create a fresh autosave, then using Continue on the next launch.

- Corrected the fixes-only in-game fullscreen freeze. Runtime confirmation showed the UI thread had already completed the borderless style/layout transition and then spun forever in `RestoreApplicationDisplay` waiting for `runtime_resource_count == 0`. That wait belongs to the original physical display-mode teardown/recreation path; modern fixes fullscreen performs neither operation, and ordinary in-game resource activity can legitimately keep the counter nonzero. The wait is now retained only for fixes-off/original behavior. A fixes-on regression test enters and leaves the transition with a nonzero count and verifies it returns without mutating the count. CTest passes 4/4, and formatting/whitespace checks pass; the active frozen debugger still prevents relinking `build/Debug/gag.exe`, so interactive confirmation remains after it is stopped.

- Corrected the AMD64 load-game dialog crash at `state->comment_text[0]`. Original x86 `ArchiveCommentDialogState` deliberately doubles as the custom preview control's GDI state: control offsets `+0x80/+0x84` alias dialog `output_1/output_2`. Reconstructing these as independently widened views made `CustomControlState::comment_text` read another native field (observed `-1` from dialog selection setup). The portable model now gives each archive dialog a dedicated `CustomControlState`, explicitly connects its archive/comment buffers to `output_1/output_2`, stores an owner back-reference, passes the nested control state to the preview HWND, and returns the owning dialog state for command handling. The x86 storage trick is no longer required to preserve its behavior. Fixes-on/fixes-off tests and CTest pass 4/4; formatting and whitespace checks pass. The active debugger still locks `build/Debug/gag.exe`, so production relinking and interactive load confirmation remain after the process is stopped.

- Corrected the AMD64 main-menu-exit/autosave crash in `ClassifyRuntimeMediaData`. The exit path packages the serialized `START.CFG` through `WriteCommentCdfPackage`; its fourth argument is a `ScriptTextBuffer { length, capacity, data }`, but the CDF reconstruction still decoded it as `uint32_t[3]`. On AMD64, element 2 read only the low DWORD of the widened `data` field (and in fact straddled native layout padding), producing the observed truncated `0x16130010` pointer before classification read `+4`. The package API and adapter now accept `ScriptTextBuffer *` and use typed `length`/`data` fields. Tests exercise the package using a real native pointer and CTest passes 4/4; formatting and whitespace checks pass. The production source compiles, but the active debugger currently locks `build/Debug/gag.exe`, so its final link and interactive exit confirmation remain after the user stops the process.

- Restored high-color cursor asset selection after the AMD64 host-state split. The fixes-only color constant was already correctly set to `ModernWindowsColorMode::rgb565_16`, and runtime graphics created a 16-bit surface, but `InitializeGagApplication` determines the script selector from `GraphicsHostInitializationResult::bits_per_pixel`. `InitializeRuntimeGraphics` updated only the canonical `RuntimeGameHostContext` copy after the former x86 aggregate was separated, leaving the returned result at zero and therefore leaving script-root selector `+0x824` at indexed/low-resolution mode. Runtime initialization now publishes the selected depth to both parts of its return contract. Tests cover both 16-bit and 8-bit publication; AMD64 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass. Interactive high-resolution cursor confirmation remains.

- Corrected the invisible AMD64 main-menu software cursor. Scene-selection and pointer-position code cast the canonical `RuntimeResourceObject` to a separately padded `RuntimeSceneRecord` whose x86 offsets ceased to match once `DisplaySceneDescriptor::pixels` widened. Pointer hit testing remained functional, but cursor scene coordinates/hotspot fields were read and written at the wrong native offsets. These paths now use `RuntimeResourceObject::{backend,type_flags,scene_identifier,x,y,requested_width,requested_height}` directly, and tree-created scene identifiers retain full pointer width. The related `RuntimeLockRecord` prefix now matches the resource object's native pointer fields so owner/recursion locking remains aligned; compile-time offset assertions guard every shared prefix field and the obsolete scene overlay was removed. AMD64 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass; interactive main-menu cursor confirmation remains.

- Corrected an AMD64 wave-output metadata overlap reported as clicking at the start of the third intro movie. `InitializeRuntimeWaveOutMixer` retained the x86 block layout `[18-byte WAVEFORMATEX][32-byte WAVEHDR][PCM]`, placing PCM at `+0x32`. On AMD64, `WAVEHDR` is 48 bytes and pointer-aligned, so WinMM header writes overlapped the first 16 PCM bytes. Output blocks now calculate an aligned native `WAVEHDR` offset and place PCM after `sizeof(WAVEHDR)`; allocation size and the second-block stride follow that calculated layout. Regression tests verify native alignment, non-overlapping PCM ranges, and architecture-derived allocation size. AMD64 Debug builds, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass; interactive confirmation of the intro transition remains.

- Continued the AMD64 fixed-image separation after the first playable intro reached bitmap capture. `CaptureGameBitmap` was still reading width, height, framebuffer, and palette through original x86 offsets in `ApplicationState::game_context`; after native pointer widening those offsets no longer named the recovered fields, and the palette read crashed in `CreateIndexedBitmap`. Bitmap capture now reads `RuntimeGameHostContext` directly. The same audit removed every remaining raw `ApplicationState::game_context + offset` access: keyboard forwarding uses the capture HWND, layout updates use the typed host offsets, save/load/display waits use `runtime_resource_count`, activation and screenshot naming use the typed current-scene/active-region globals, resource construction uses the typed palette pointer, and resource-directory updates use their separate storage. Display reset now clears whole native pointers and the actual typed 21-DWORD logical state instead of low DWORD aliases and disconnected scratch storage. AMD64 Debug builds, both fixes-on/fixes-off startup tests, resource tests, and XTET tests pass 4/4; the next step is an interactive run past the intro/menu transition.

- As the first deliberate post-checkpoint portability change, removed all 2,032 source-level `__fastcall` annotations from GAG production code, CDF code, tests, and the XTET loader, and removed the `XTET_ABI` macro from the reconstructed DLL API/exports. Function definitions, declarations, injectable callback types, test doubles, host callback slots, and both sides of the GAG/XTET boundary now use the compiler's default C++ calling convention. Win32 production/test/DLL targets compile and CTest passes 4/4. The aggregate build reached only an unrelated `LNK1168` because a running `build/Debug/xtet_loader.exe` was locked and could not be overwritten. No x64 configuration or build was attempted at the user's request. `WINAPI`/`CALLBACK` annotations remain because they are Windows-owned API contracts, not recovered game fastcall conventions. The CMake 32-bit guard and pointer/layout portability work remain separate next steps.
- The known playable, end-to-end completable checkpoint is tagged `completable-2026-08-20`. It includes the user-verified completable game state, subsequent fixes-only resource lookup and FetchContent zlib work, and the assembly-portability cleanup. This tag is the stable baseline before larger portability changes.
- Removed the only inline assembly in the reconstructed source and the false hidden-register ABI built around it. `SetRuntimeScriptProperty` previously copied incoming ESI with compiler-specific assembly and passed it through `_with_loop_register`/`_with_stack_value` helpers as a ninth resource-constructor argument. Raw GAG.EXE instructions prove `ConstructRuntimeResourceObject` has exactly eight parameters (ECX path, EDX scene identifier, six stack arguments; `RET 0x18`); the ESI saved by `SelectRuntimeResource` lies beyond those arguments and is merely preserved caller state. Resource selection, tree/pointer rebuilds, right-button handling, and tests now call the real eight-argument constructor directly. At the completable checkpoint, the genuine x86 `__fastcall` boundary remained intentionally intact; it was removed only by the subsequent explicit portability change recorded above. Ghidra's `SelectRuntimeResource` comment is corrected and saved. Win32 Debug builds and CTest passes 4/4; a source audit finds no remaining inline assembly, naked functions, compiler intrinsics, or built-in assembly dependencies outside generated/third-party code.
- Replaced the crude root-level zlib source/header manifest with CMake `FetchContent`. It downloads the official zlib 1.3.2 release archive, verifies SHA-256 `bb329a0a2cd0274d05519d61c667c062e06990d72e125ee2dfa8de64f0119d16`, disables zlib shared libraries, tests, and installation, and links the upstream `ZLIB::ZLIBSTATIC` target. A fresh population into `build/_deps` followed by the Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks passes. The pre-existing `third_party/zlib-1.3.2` tree is untracked user-local data and was deliberately left untouched; CMake no longer references it.
- Added fixes-only executable-directory resource precedence. Every requested CDF pack (including startup `Gag01.cdf` and script-driven disc changes such as `Gag02.cdf`) is checked beside `gag.exe` before the recovered installed/CD drive search. `/GAME` DLL loading similarly tries the DLL filename beside `gag.exe` before falling back to the active resource-directory path. Fixes-off retains the original lookup order. Both paths reject truncated/overflowing `MAX_PATH` candidates and have opposing tests for priority and fallback; Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, tab scan, and LF scan pass.
- Traced Gary's bedroom nail and dream variants in `GR.CIN::GRBD/DREAM3`. Exactly two inventory objects can be hung on the nail: `MBust` (the bust collected in `MR.CIN::MR11`) and `Short` (the shorts collected through `XXX.CIN::XXX_13`). They are mutually exclusive; hanging one returns the other to inventory, and either can be picked back up. The third relevant state is leaving the nail empty. Only menu dream 3 reads these flags: empty plays `DR_SLEEP1` + `DR_POOR` (`GR6004.mov`, `GR6001.mov`), bust plays `DR_SLEEP2` + `DR_GOOD1` (`GR6005.mov`, `GR6002.mov`), and shorts play `DR_SLEEP2` + `DR_GOOD2` (`GR6005.mov`, `GR6003.mov`). Dream menu entries 1 and 2 do not depend on the nail.
- Verified the route to `Short`: in Margo's room, start the Realizator by opening it and using the coin (`Realiz::Work=ON`), open the notebook, choose `XXX`, then press Enter. Once `Jump::End=ON`, this loads `XXX.CIN::XXX_13`; clicking the shorts at `(285,250)-(350,295)` adds them to inventory, removes `Pants`, and enables `MROOM::GoOut`.

- Completed a conservative section-reachability audit over all 57 extracted CFG/CIN files (47 case-insensitive resource names), using the recovered GAG parser semantics: filenames are case-insensitive, section names are exact and first-match wins, leading-`*` property statements including continuations are disabled, and all conditional branches are counted. From the three GAG.EXE roots (`Start.cfg::CFG`, `NewGame.cfg::CFG`, and `Credits.cfg::CFG`), 2,230 of 2,596 first-match sections are reachable and 366 are not. Of the 366, 90 are empty/structural `CFG` or `END` sentinels and eight belong to the separately invoked `AUTORUN.CFG` installer UI; this leaves 268 nonstructural first-match game sections unreachable. Another 18 later duplicate definitions (17 in `MR.CIN`, one `IVIEW.CIN::TTZONE`) are shadowed by first-match lookup, for 286 nonstructural dead game definitions in total. Major abandoned groups are the incomplete second Margo-room navigation map in `MR.CIN`; the disabled fly-minigame title/attract section `FLY.CIN::TITR` (which also names absent `FlyNet.bmp`); the disabled alternate tic-tac-toe opponent path `XO.CIN::FIRST/GETPOS/ZERO!/SET1..SET9` (its required `P01..P24` conditions are also commented, while shipped play uses random `ZERO`); the disabled moving-crocodile overlay `CH-FLY.CIN::CROKODILE`; unused furnace-control alternatives `HG.CIN::HGX2/HGX3`; the commented phase dispatcher `HG.CIN::PHASE` superseded by conditional sublocations; the incomplete two-room `HALL.CIN::HL14/HL15` island (missing both background assets); the old broken maze overlay `MZ.CIN::VIEWMAP_OLD`; the disabled `ISLAND.CIN::SP29b` NPC state plus orphan `SP29c`; a fax debug/shortcut section `GRFX.CIN::GRFX1`; and multiple obsolete helper/zone fragments. There are 31 unreachable `COMMENT` sections containing cut spoken/subtitle lines, including the omitted generic refusal `NEG.rus:6` ("Even don't think about it"), two island lines, numerous Margo-room observations, and an unused laundry-elevator description. Twenty-nine existing media assets are referenced exclusively by dead game sections (33 including the four autorun UI bitmaps); this extends the earlier literal-orphan audit because most still have script literals. No source or original data was changed.

- The reachability audit found one additional confirmed live maze typo: `MZ.CIN:1392` section `09012` instantiates nonexistent `09012xy`, while the immediately following helper is `[0901xy]` and publishes the exact `(263,362)` map position. This follows the same pattern as every other special-orientation `*xy` helper: the original inline `Plan` event is commented and the helper supplies it instead. Therefore the typo prevents this one orientation from updating/publishing its position when the maze map command is used. The previously known `11010 -> 11011` bad turn remains the other confirmed live section target defect. The other live-source unresolved names were classified: ten empty `/LOADNOFADE:/PLOAD` forms intentionally omit a transition animation; zero-area map links such as `SP8M -> SP2`, empty-state placeholders such as `WC01 -> FULL1` and `MR31 -> MR31b`, and story-state-incompatible disc copies (`YEARD` missing late `SP14`, `CASTEL` missing early `MSLEEP/MARKIZ2`) do not form reachable gameplay failures. `GRTV.CIN::GR2` also contains stale bare `CHIKEN` and `GOTOMR` sublocations; explicit navigation replaces `GOTOMR`, while the missing `CHIKEN` helper removes only the optional upward chicken interaction from that one room orientation. `GR.CIN::EXPAND -> GR72` is a stale/missing conditional overlay with no corresponding shipped `GR7xx` still asset. No correction was made because this task was an audit.

- Audited all 2,646 extracted non-script archive files for literal CFG/CIN references. Only two unique payloads have no script reference: `VERSION.TXT`, which is not unused because GAG.EXE hardcodes `Version.txt` for disc/edition discovery, and `I-ITEMS.256`, a valid 640x78 8-bit red filmstrip-style inventory bar duplicated on both discs. GAG.EXE has no embedded `I-ITEMS` string, while active inventory scripts select `INV256.BMP` for indexed color and `I-ITEMS.BMP` for high color, making `I-ITEMS.256` the one confirmed wholly orphaned visual asset. Seven additional unique media assets occur only on leading-`*` disabled declarations: `DN0600A.WAV` (worm sound; active stale `/PLAY`/`STOP` commands remain), `GR6002.BMP` (open-box overlay in a state that is never set), `I-ICON0.BMP` and `I-ICON1.BMP` (superseded inventory show/hide icons), `I-JUMP.BMP` (superseded jump-task icon), `MR22-01.MOV` (book animation in an otherwise unreferenced helper section), and `MR23K1.FLC` (unused meat overlay in the crocodile room). These are abandoned/disabled content candidates, not proof that restoring them matches shipped gameplay. No assets or source were changed.

- Audited all 57 extracted CFG/CIN files against the recovered GAG.EXE lexer, property/scope/opcode tables, section lookup, and control-boundary scanner. The remembered strict-parser failures are mostly tolerated original data: `GR.CIN:717` is a separator missing its leading `*`; `MRMG.CIN:295` is an incomplete bare `sublocation`; 19 `/RATIO` scopes omit `:` but whitespace is accepted; 240 maze `/F:BACKGROUND` tokens are unknown legacy/editor tokens and reduce to zero while `/F:PERMANENT` remains effective; and 21 `/SCEND` misspellings are unknown no-ops for intended `/CSEND`. The `/SCEND` sites terminate at the next section boundary or occur after branch exits in normal reachable states; the only trailing command they can swallow is `MZ.CIN::ARMOR`'s fallback `/PEXIT`, but its two entry paths guarantee one of the preceding conditions. Two `/F:LOADONLY` misspellings (`GRTV.CIN::GR20` and `ISLAND.CIN::GUN`) omit the real `LOAD_ONLY` bit and therefore do alter WAV construction/configuration relative to 65 correctly spelled peers, potentially changing cue timing. One unequivocal live navigation defect is `MZ.CIN:1788`: cell `11010` turns with `T12` to nonexistent `11011`; every analogous two-orientation maze cell and the reciprocal `11012 -> T11 -> 11010` transition prove the target should be `11012`. In game, pressing the bottom/down arrow at map position `(335,379)` while in view `11010` plays `Mzrun12k.mov`, fails to load the opposite dead-end view (`Mzp1t.bmp`), and rebuilds/snaps back to the same `Mzp2.bmp` view. The reverse `11012 -> 11010` turn works. On `MZMAP.bmp`, this is the southern tip of the short vertical dead-end immediately to the right of the large red question mark near the bottom center; forward from `11010` travels north/up the map toward `(341,351)`. `MZMAP` records only the shared X/Y position and draws the same `MZPOS.flc` marker for both orientations, so the map does not expose the failed facing change, although its corridor layout can make the ineffective arrow more noticeable. This prevents looking toward that dead-end wall but does not block traversal or completion. The many missing `MR6/MR10/...` targets and duplicate `MR*M` sections belong to an unreferenced alternate room-map block, not live room navigation. No original data or source was changed during the audit.

- End-to-end acceptance milestone: the user completed the recompiled game and confirmed it is fully completable. The possible repeat fly-catching discrepancy is deferred: static content shows the same cursor and difficulty-menu logic on every invocation, while the remembered original behavior may instead have been failure to redraw the reused load-only difficulty highlights. No speculative emulation was added.

- Investigated the remembered invisible cursor on the repeat fly-catching minigame. Original `GR.CIN` permits replay whenever `Fly::Open=ON`; opening the table fly once makes that flag permanent, while later uses of the captured fly only exclude `Inventory::Fly` and do not reset `Fly::Open` or `Fly::Pick_Up`. Original `FLY.CIN` contains no first-versus-repeat cursor condition: every invocation defines `None=FLSPY2.BMP` for the general play field and `Spy=FLSPY.BMP` for catchable fly zones through the same `GO -> GAME -> GAME_Z` path. Persistent `FLY_OBJ` state does create one intentional replay difference: after the first win leaves `FLY_DEAD=0`, the next successful hit immediately satisfies `l_BONNE_APPETITE`, so only one fly is required. GAG.EXE's visual-object and command-definition paths also contain no story-state cursor branch. The user's memory of a completely invisible second-run cursor may reflect an original resource/palette-lifetime glitch, but it is not established by static evidence; do not emulate it without a controlled original-game comparison showing whether `None`, `Spy`, or both fail to render. No source change was made.

- Investigated the apparently stalled late-game hangar crocodile interaction directly from original `HG.CIN`. It is intentional puzzle progression rather than a reconstructed runtime defect. First use of `Pica` advances `HANGAR::NC` from 0 to 1 without removing the item. Three ordinary crocodile clicks then traverse `CUREAT2` (USM/pointer), `CUREAT3` (TM/hand), and `CUREAT1` (EM/magnifying glass), ending at `HANGAR::NC=4`. `CUREAT4` deliberately overlays the scene with the default/hidden `MM` cursor and has no ordinary-click continuation. The required next action is to select and use `Pica` on the crocodile a second time; only that event excludes it from inventory and loads `KILL`. The subsequent full-screen `Eat5` forward-cursor click sets `HANGAR::GoOut=ON` and advances to the escape movie/location. No source change was made.

- Corrected the near-endgame crocodile-escape parser crash as an original-fidelity temporary-buffer recovery error. GAG.EXE `ParseScriptIntegerExpression` (`0x0040F4F0`) passes a distinct 0x20-byte value buffer to `EvaluateScriptParameter` and reads only its first DWORD after successful integer validation; source instead passed the address of its 4-byte scalar result. `EvaluateScriptParameter` materializes the parameter before checking its type, so a non-integer parameter wrote a full string across the caller's stack and corrupted the saved parser pointer, producing the observed null-parser write on failure rollback. The integer-expression PARAM path now uses the original-sized temporary. The same confirmed error existed in `ParseImageFlag` (`0x0040E580`) and is corrected there as well. Regression tests exercise both paths through the real evaluator with deliberately mismatched string parameters. Ghidra comments are synchronized and saved; the Win32 Debug production executable builds, fixes-on/fixes-off CTest passes 4/4, and `check-format` passes. Interactive replay of the crocodile sequence remains for the user.

- Investigated the apparently missing castle minesweeper (`Mino`) using the user's latest `vacuum` save (`GAG002.GSF`) and the original extracted scripts. The same save also hides the item in original GAG, ruling out a reconstructed scene/rendering discrepancy. Decoding its embedded `START.CFG` shows `Mino::Pick_Up=OFF`, no `Mino` in `Inventory`, and `MROOM::GoOut=OFF`. `KR.CIN::KS08` deliberately instantiates `GET_MINO` only when `Mino::Pick_Up=OFF` and `MROOM::GoOut=ON`. The save remains recoverable: `Jump::End=ON`, `Realiz::Work=ON`, and `XXX::Pic13=ON`, so entering the XXX program from the notebook in Margo's room loads `XXX_13`; picking the shorts in its `(285,250)-(350,295)` zone sets `MROOM::GoOut=ON`, after which returning to `KS08` exposes the minesweeper. The apparent inability to leave Margo's room is part of the same deliberate sequence: `MR8a`, the route to the open balcony/boat, exists only while `Jump::End=OFF`, while late re-entry through `KR.CIN::GOTOMR` loads `MR3a`, whose `LOCKMR` closes the main door. The `XXX_13` pickup event ends by preloading `MR.CIN::GOOUT`, so completing it is the intended late-game escape. No source or save modification was made.

- Investigated the sewer rat-shooting minigame and found no coordinate or reconstructed-event defect. Runtime traces at native 640x480 and fixes-only 3x Window scaling showed that pointer coordinates landed inside the intended `z_RATxx` rectangles, priority 100 beat the broad direction zones, left-button release queued the expected `Go_O`/`KEYUP` record, and the corresponding `e_BEATxx` event matched. Held shots advanced `i_RGC0` through frames 15-50; `RATS.CIN` deliberately treats only frames 8-17 as the aligned phase and places miss-impact animations elsewhere for the other phase bands. The user achieved a hit during the held-shot test, consistent with this mechanic. GAG.EXE raw code confirms `PHASE` queries the backend frame counter at `+0x99C`. No behavioral change was made; temporary tracing and its log were removed, and a clean Win32 Debug verification follows.

- Implemented fixes-only per-user persistence for all original main-menu settings. Ghidra confirms `SaveRuntimeSettings` (`0x0041D060`) masks `ApplicationState::flags` with `0x02001020` (Full Screen `0x20`, Sound `0x1000`, subtitles/comments `0x02000000`) and writes DWORD value `set` under `HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\ZES't Corp.\\GAG`; `LoadInstallationRegistrySettings` (`0x0041EDF0`) reads the same value and mask. The rebuilt manifested process cannot write that read-only HKLM key and receives no legacy virtualization. Under `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`, shutdown now creates/updates `HKCU\\SOFTWARE\\ZES't Corp.\\GAG\\set`, while startup continues reading installation version/path from HKLM, prefers the valid HKCU settings value, and falls back to the original HKLM value for migration when no per-user value exists. Fixes-off retains the exact original HKLM `KEY_ALL_ACCESS` save and load behavior. Opposing tests cover HKCU precedence, HKLM fallback, per-user key creation, and unchanged fixes-off access. Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, and `check-format` pass; interactive toggle-exit-relaunch confirmation remains for the user.

- Corrected the rejected/translucent Fly-to-microwave interaction as an original-fidelity writable-global field recovery error in `HandleRuntimeRightButtonDown` (`0x00423CA0`). Runtime tracing showed drag eligibility itself was correct, but the active region remained the priority-201 `Inventory_Close_Zone` rather than the microwave because the inventory overlay was never removed. Raw instructions at `0x00423E8A` pass address `0x00480158` to `FindRuntimeTreeDescendantIdentityByName`; this address is `ScriptRuntimeRoot` (`0x0047F910`) plus `0x848`, the live `parser_value_0848` populated by the script `class` property (for inventory, `INVENTORY_PACK`). Source incorrectly flattened the initially zero BSS bytes into a local empty string, so descendant lookup always failed. Drag startup now reads the live root field and removes the matching inventory subtree exactly as the original does. Focused tests distinguish a populated class name from an empty field. Temporary inventory tracing and its log were removed, the Ghidra comment is synchronized and saved, and the Win32 Debug build, fixes-on/fixes-off CTest 4/4, and `check-format` pass; interactive Fly-to-microwave confirmation remains for the user.

- Corrected the fly-catching minigame's garbled mixed-format sound as an original-fidelity active-slot scan inversion in `CreateSoundHandle` (`0x00401820`). The minigame combines `FLFLY.WAV` (8-bit mono, 11025 Hz) with `FLY1.WAV`/`FLY2.WAV` (16-bit stereo, 11025 Hz). Raw instructions at `0x004018BD..0x004018D8` advance while sound slots are inactive and stop on the first active slot; when any sound is active, the new format is converted into the existing waveOut format. Source instead stopped on the first inactive slot, so every differing WAV format rebuilt the mixer and destroyed existing handles. The scan now matches GAG, while the later allocation scan remains a distinct search for an inactive slot. Focused tests prove no-active-slot format changes rebuild the mixer and active mixed-format changes preserve the existing slot, calculate conversion flags, and allocate the next free handle. Ghidra's function comment is synchronized and saved. Win32 Debug build, fixes-on/fixes-off CTest 4/4, and `check-format` pass; interactive fly-scene audio confirmation remains for the user.

- Corrected the next-screen PCM garbage/crash as an original-fidelity WAV chunk-literal recovery error in `ConstructRuntimeResourceObject` (`0x00424EC0`). Raw instructions at `0x004252B1..0x00425342` scan from loaded resource data `+0x24` for the four bytes stored at `0x0044216C`, then queue `chunk + 8` with the DWORD size at `chunk + 4`. Those bytes are ASCII `data` (`64 61 74 61`), but source searched for integer `1` (`01 00 00 00`), so it stopped on an ordinary PCM header field and queued header/unrelated memory as audio. Source now scans for the exact four-byte `data` chunk ID. The focused constructor test places an integer-1 decoy before the actual data chunk and verifies only the real payload and size are queued. Ghidra labels `0x0044216C` as `s_wave_data_chunk_id`, documents the scan, and is saved. Win32 Debug production build, fixes-on/fixes-off CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass; interactive replay through the reported screen remains for the user.

- Corrected the post-New-Game-sequence `0xC0000005` as an original-fidelity four-argument fastcall recovery error in `ActivateRuntimeTreeWithNotifications` (`0x00426560`). The runtime stack proved `copy_string` received `0xFFFFFFFF` through `FindOrCreateRuntimeTreeParserContext` creation text after a global-load opcode. Raw staging at `0x00426581..0x00426594` keeps both incoming stack arguments distinct: after saving registers, `[ESP+0x14]` is original argument 4 and is pushed as `CreateRuntimeTreeNode`'s creation context, while `[ESP+0x18]` after the two staging pushes is original argument 3 and is loaded into EDX as the parent selector. Source instead duplicated argument 3 into both positions, so the legitimate global-root parent sentinel `0xFFFFFFFF` became a string pointer. The function now forwards `parent_selector` and `creation_context` separately; focused tests use distinct addresses and explicitly verify `parent_selector == 0xFFFFFFFF` with null creation context. The temporary user breakpoint block and attempted self-recording logger/CMake dependency were completely removed. Ghidra's prototype and plate comment are synchronized and saved. Win32 Debug production build succeeds; fixes-on/fixes-off CTest 3/3 and `check-format` pass. Interactive replay through the formerly crashing sequence remains for the user.

- Completed a GAG.EXE initialized string-pointer-table audit using an inline Ghidra instruction/memory pass enabled by `GHIDRA_MCP_ALLOW_SCRIPTS=1`. The audit enumerated 41 instructions, 22 unique referenced ASCII string slots, and 36 unique function/slot consumer pairs; separate UTF-16 and indexed-table passes found only CRT `(null)` data and no program-owned indexed string tables. Every recovered consumer was compared against its exact slot target. Besides the already-corrected `NewGame.cfg` site, two additional mismatches were confirmed in `GagMainWindowProcedure`: `0x0041DA3E` and `0x0041E43F` both load shared slot `0x00440900 -> 0x0043F100` (`AutoSave.cdf`), but source appended adjacent-slot `CREDITS`. These are respectively the Resume Game autosave path and the shutdown state-save path. Both now use a single `auto_save_file_name` constant, also shared with startup autosave detection at `0x0041F0CE`; the Resume Game regression expectation is `D:\\GAG\\AutoSave.cdf`. No other program-owned initialized string-pointer mismatch was found. Ghidra comments at all three `AutoSave.cdf` consumers are synchronized and the program is saved. Win32 Debug build, fixes-on/fixes-off CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass.

- Corrected and runtime-verified the Start Game black-screen root cause as original-fidelity pointer-table recovery, with no `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` guard and no inferred extension fallback. `GagMainWindowProcedure` instruction `0x0041D9A7` loads the pointer-table slot at `0x004408EC`; that slot contains `0x0043F138`, which points to the embedded string `NewGame.cfg`. The adjacent slot at `0x004408F0` instead points to the separate `NEWGAME` string at `0x0043F130`; the earlier reconstruction flattened the indirect read to this neighboring literal. The source now copies `NewGame.cfg` unconditionally. The archive's exact extracted spelling is `NEWGAME.CFG`, and focused real-CDF tests prove lookup is case-insensitive (`NewGame.cfg` and `NEWGAME.CFG` both return flags `0x14`) while extensionless `NEWGAME` returns zero. The user confirmed the corrected path starts the game. All investigation diagnostics and the superseded fixes-only workaround were removed; Ghidra's disassembly comment records the slot-level evidence. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass.

- Fixed and interactively verified fixes-only Full Screen input end to end. `DIALOG.CFG` exposes `e_FULL` as a level-triggered event whose `/MESSAGE::2010` crosses threads with synchronous `SendMessage`; the receiver calls `ReplyMessage` before completing the modern borderless transition, so the script can reevaluate the same press and issue another synchronous 2010 while the UI thread is still inside the first callback. That second send deadlocked the script worker, leaving Full Screen with raw Win32 input but no hover or clicks. Duplicate 2010 suppression now occurs at the script-message sending boundary, once per physical press and rearmed on the next button-down, so the blocking send is never attempted; the receiver retains its recovered behavior. During the transition, the fixes path also preserves the physical `WM_LBUTTONUP` that the original queue gate would drop, while continuing to reject resize-generated mouse moves that would change the active game region before release. Fixes-off remains unchanged. Focused tests cover sender-side duplicate suppression/rearming, fixes-off pass-through, and the transition move/release/move queue sequence. The user confirmed fullscreen hover and clicks work. All temporary intro automation, file/link/event tracing, the generated log, mouse-capture experiment, and disproven completion-generation experiment were removed. Clean Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass.

- Implemented fixes-only modern scaled Full Screen while preserving the original menu command/deferred-transition state machine and persisted flag. `DetectAlternateDisplayMode` now exposes capability bit `0x4000` under fixes, while application-facing display-mode setters return success without invoking DirectDraw or `ChangeDisplaySettings`. Full Screen uses a non-topmost borderless popup on the window's current monitor, a centered capture child, black parent letterboxing, and a saved framed rectangle for exact Window-mode restoration; startup directly in persisted Full Screen preloads the prior Window-mode rectangle (or a centered fallback). The fixes-only source enum supports `preserve_aspect`, `stretch`, and default `integer` scaling, with aspect-preserving downscale below 640x480. GDI presentation uses `StretchBlt`/`COLORONCOLOR` in Full Screen and retains unscaled `BitBlt` in Window mode; clears and repaint invalidation cover scaled viewport and black bars. Capture-child mouse coordinates are mapped and clamped back to 640x480 for the DLL callback, pointer state, main-window forwarding, and queued events, while letterbox boundaries remain outside the game surface. Focused tests cover all viewport modes, small-monitor fallback, menu capability/commands, mode-switch suppression, monitor layout, exact restore, persisted-fullscreen startup, scaled copy/clear, and mouse mapping. Win32 Debug build and CTest pass 3/3 for fixes-on and fixes-off; interactive multi-monitor/menu transition confirmation remains for the user.

- Removed the thin horizontal bars in fixes-enabled Window mode. Both compatibility-only `AdjustWindowRect` calls incorrectly passed `TRUE` for the menu argument even though the top-level GAG window has no menu. Windows therefore reserved an extra menu row, producing a 500-pixel-high client in the test geometry and centering the 480-pixel capture child between roughly 10-pixel top/bottom gaps. The calls now pass `FALSE`, preserving the exact 640x480 client; the 100%-scale test frame changes from 648x534 to 648x514. Startup, layout refresh, restore sizing, and saved-position visibility tests cover the corrected geometry. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass; interactive visual confirmation remains for the user.

- Corrected the sound-toggle callback crash as original-fidelity behavior, with no `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` guard. Raw GAG control flow in `EnableRuntimeSubsystem` (`0x00420BC0`) and `DisableRuntimeSubsystem` (`0x00420BE0`) directly calls `ToggleRuntimeSoundState` (`0x004010A0`) after changing scene-control bit 2. Source instead called an artificial injectable function pointer that production never initialized, so menu message 2020/command `0x8850` could jump to garbage and surface through the Win32 window-procedure boundary as `0xC000041D`. Both paths now call the recovered function directly; the artificial global, setter, and counter callback were removed. Focused tests prove enable and disable each complement the actual toggle-state storage once and repeated same-state calls do not toggle. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass; interactive sound-toggle confirmation remains for the user.

- Corrected the confirmed main-menu hover/toggle root cause as original-fidelity behavior, with no `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` guard. `ParseScriptValueToken` (`0x0040F2C0`) uses `CMP 1; SBB; AND 0xFFFFFFDF; ADD 0x20` after both `PARAM` and `SVALUE` lookup. The failed path wraps to `0xFFFFFFFF`, but source returned zero. Omitted `MENU_3S` parameters such as `p_clsZ` were therefore accepted as valid empty event names; those unconditional links repeatedly reset `MM::SEL`, producing intermittent hover highlights and hover-like setting changes. Both failed expansion paths now return `0xFFFFFFFF`, and tests cover missing `PARAM` and `SVALUE` values. The investigation also retained a separate valid adjacent-field correction: raw instructions in `SelectPointerRegionScene` (`0x004237F0`) and `UpdateRuntimePointerRegion` (`0x00423FA0`, `0x00424154`) read the command mask at `ScriptObjectState +0x47C`, not the active-field mask at `+0x480`; opposing-mask tests guard that distinction. All temporary menu tracing and its 303605-byte log were removed. Ghidra's function comment is corrected and saved. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass; interactive main-menu confirmation remains for the user.

- Completed the writable-global storage-overlap audit and corrected every confirmed artificial split. The inventory covered 79 writable named GAG globals; 40 reside in the already-modeled `0x0047EF60..0x00480CDC` graphics-host allocation. Three additional original backing groups are now represented directly: the 0x84-byte display-scene host at `0x0043EFD8`, the 0x488-byte display-mode host at `0x0043EA98`, and the 1025th 0x34-byte sound-slot overflow beginning at `0x0043E048` that aliases the following sound subsystem globals and embedded `SoundClassName`. The display-mode model also restores the deliberate `0x0043EAD8` tail/enumeration-cursor alias and removes the invented palette-surface-depth mirror in favor of the current mode's original `+0x28` field. The remaining graphics-host `REP STOSD` ranges in `ShutdownRuntimeDisplay`, `ShutdownGraphicsHost`, `ResetRuntimeDisplayState`, and `ResetRuntimeSession` were checked against raw Ghidra output and already match the shared allocation model. Regression tests exercise cross-view display-scene dimensions, sound overflow side effects, and destructive display-mode tail/cursor reuse. No unresolved confirmed overlap remains; adjacent globals without allocation or cross-write evidence remain independent. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, and `git diff --check` pass.

- Corrected the 16-bit cursor-asset selector as an original-fidelity storage-alias fix, with no `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` guard around the correction. Cursor definitions in `Start.cfg` pair `K_*.256:0` with the smoother `K_*.bmp:1`, and `ParseScriptFileValue` (`0x0040CDA0`) compares those qualifiers against script-root field `+0x824`. GAG.EXE uses the single global pointer at `0x0043E2B0` in both `SetScriptRuntimeRootIfValid` (`0x00404970`) and `SetActiveObjectField0824` (`0x00404980`); source had incorrectly split the latter onto an unrelated null `active_object`, so the post-initialization `>8 bpp` write was discarded and selector zero retained the `.256` cursors. `set_active_object_field_0824` now writes `ScriptRuntimeRoot::state_value_0824` through `script_runtime_root`, and the artificial global/test seam were removed. A regression test proves the setter observes the root published through the original setter. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass; interactive cursor appearance remains for the user to confirm.

- Corrected the title-menu exit-loop root cause as an original-fidelity reconstruction fix, with no `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` guard. In `ExecuteScriptCommands` (`0x00421530`), successful `/PLOAD` and `/SPLOAD` branches jump directly to `0x00423178/0x0042317D`, bypassing the common saved-parser-cursor restore at `0x00422E5A`; source instead restarted the outer loop after restoring the pre-opcode cursor. Consequently `MENU_SET` repeatedly executed `/PLOAD MENU_MAIN`, recreating the main menu with `MM::EXIT` still active and producing the exit-cursor flicker. Successful loads now use a distinct restart-and-commit-cursor disposition, allowing the script to advance to `/WAIT` and `/PEXIT`. The superseded fixes-only synthetic-enter suppression and all diagnostic tracing were removed. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. Clean process 23252 is left open at the post-click state for interactive confirmation.

- Fixed fixes-enabled Window mode jumping to the top-left after minimize/restore. The recovered `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING` path casts `WINDOWPOS` to `SecondaryWindowLayout`; its original layout helper overwrites `x/y` from `desktop_window_rect` and clears `SWP_NOMOVE`. In compatibility Window mode that rectangle begins at `0,0`, so restore was forced there. The fixes-only branch now preserves the incoming Windows-managed `x/y` and `SWP_NOMOVE`, while still enforcing the fixed 648x514 test outer size and repositioning only the capture child. Full Screen and fixes-OFF behavior are unchanged. A focused regression test covers preserved restore coordinates/flags. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. Interactive restore verification remains for the user because repeated diagnostic launches were stopped at their request.

- Added fixes-only Window-mode position persistence. On a normal shutdown in Window mode, the top-level window's left/top coordinates are stored as the `WindowPosition` binary `POINT` under `HKCU\\SOFTWARE\\ZES't Corp.\\GAG`; Full Screen shutdown and fixes-OFF do not use this compatibility value. A later Window-mode launch uses the saved coordinates directly in `CreateWindowExA`, so there is no post-creation jump. Missing, malformed, and off-screen saved rectangles fall back to `CW_USEDEFAULT`, with `MonitorFromRect(..., MONITOR_DEFAULTTONULL)` validating the current monitor topology. Tests cover missing, valid, off-screen, saved, fixes-only, and original-fidelity behavior. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. Ghidra is unchanged because this is explicitly non-original compatibility behavior.

- Removed fixes-enabled Window-mode top-level position manipulation. `InitializeGagApplication` creates the fixed 648x514 test outer window with `CW_USEDEFAULT`, but skips the original post-`ShowWindow` `SetWindowPos`; `UpdateApplicationWindowLayout` retains the visible fixed-frame style and internal capture-child placement without centering or otherwise positioning the top-level HWND. Full Screen and fixes-OFF/original-fidelity paths are unchanged. Focused tests prove fixes-enabled Window startup emits no top-level position event and later layout only positions the rendering child. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. The earlier interactive process 12100 has exited.

- Restored the normal startup sequence after the menu-input investigation. Removed the temporary `FREEGAG_SKIP_STARTUP_MOVIES` and `FREEGAG_MENU_TRACE` production definitions, the first-frame movie completion override, all menu/input file logging calls and helpers, empty Debug probe blocks, and both generated `freegag-menu.log` files. The confirmed dispatcher binding and earlier reconstruction fixes remain intact. The clean Win32 Debug build succeeds, CTest passes 3/3, and `check-format` passes.

- Changed the default-ON modern Window-mode startup placement as requested. `InitializeGagApplication` uses `AdjustWindowRect` to preserve the exact 640x480 client and fixed 648x514 test outer size, but passes `CW_USEDEFAULT` for both initial x/y coordinates instead of centering from desktop metrics. Later explicit game layout transitions are unchanged, and fixes-OFF retains original placement. Both startup variants and CTest 3/3 pass. Ghidra is unchanged because this is explicitly non-original compatibility behavior.

- User build-directory constraint: use only the standard root `build/` CMake/VS Code cache for routine Win32 Debug builds and verification. Do not create alternate acceptance or dependency-specific build directories unless explicitly requested. The obsolete generated `build-acceptance/` and `build-zlib-acceptance/` trees were removed; `.gitignore` remains untouched.

- Corrected the title-menu pointer-region root cause in `ParseRuntimeTreeLink84` (`0x0040AAC0`). Raw Ghidra instructions prove `/POS` (`0x0B000000`) stores `x + width` and `y + height`, while `/RECT` (`0x02000000`) stores its third and fourth values as absolute right/bottom edges. Source had these semantics reversed, producing impossible menu bounds such as `z_EXIT` top 70/bottom 25; only the broad fallback zones could ever win hit testing. The parser now matches the original, and focused tests assert both forms. All temporary input traces, their compile definition, the 39 MB diagnostic log, and diagnostic process were removed. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. Git-backed diff checking is temporarily unavailable because the local `.git` metadata is not recognized as a repository by Git, but direct tab/trailing-whitespace scans of the edited files are clean. The next interactive check is that title-screen menu actions now respond.

- Corrected and runtime-verified the post-intro/menu black-screen root cause in `CreateRuntimeTreeNode` (`0x00405410`). Raw Ghidra instructions at `0x00405421..0x0040542A` preserve the incoming EDX `parent_selector` and pass it to `FindRuntimeTreeNode`; source had instead passed `script_runtime_root->runtime_tree`, causing every null-parent PLOAD (including `MENU_MAIN`) to be created as a child of the current root. `RebuildRuntimeTreeResources` then correctly refused to publish it at `0x0047F8C4`, leaving the command loop with a null active identity. Source now forwards the exact selector and tests observe both null and non-null identities. A bounded runtime probe followed the complete unskipped sequence and proved `MENU_MAIN` was created with null selector/parent, rebuilt as the root, executed transition `0x30002002`, opened the full 640x480 clip, composed its background and menu regions, and synchronized the full frame. After removing all probes and logs, the clean executable repeated the sequence; its visible `FlcAppClassNT` graphics child sampled 51 distinct colors with 90/99 sampled points nonblack. Clean Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, `git diff --check`, and scoped source whitespace checks pass. Clean process 26732 is intentionally left running at the menu for user inspection.

- Added the requested Window-mode focus-loss compatibility behavior. Original `GagMainWindowProcedure` (`0x0041D560`) and `GagCaptureWindowProcedure` (`0x0041E680`) both post `WM_SYSCOMMAND/SC_MINIMIZE` on inactive `WM_ACTIVATE`; with `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`, that post is now suppressed only while application flag `0x80` selects Window mode. Full Screen and fixes-OFF retain the recovered auto-minimize behavior. Dual-variant tests cover both procedures and both configurations. A stale running process locking `gag.exe` was terminated; the clean Debug production target rebuilds, CTest passes 3/3, `check-format` passes, and the source tab scan is clean. Ghidra remains unchanged because the guarded behavior is explicitly non-original compatibility code.

- Verified the reported post-logo "stall" is not an animation-completion failure. Runtime evidence showed `LOGOgag.mov` advancing through frame 360/360, publishing media flags `0x06402001`, satisfying `/WAIT:Gag`, destroying the GAG tree/resource with the resource count reaching zero, and activating/rebuilding `Start.cfg::FRONT`. The FRONT script deliberately preserves the final movie frame: `*image=i_FRONT /FILE:LOGO.bmp` is a commented line (the CFG files consistently use leading `*` for comments), while a full-screen `Main` zone waits for a click and then `/SPLOAD:dialog.cfg::MENU_LOAD_FADE`. All temporary movie skips, runtime trace definitions, focused probes, and the trace log were removed; the clean Debug executable and both startup test variants build, and CTest passes 3/3. The next runtime check is whether clicking anywhere on the final logo opens the menu; if not, trace the full-screen zone/pointer event dispatch rather than movie playback.

- User-required troubleshooting rule: each diagnosis may temporarily introduce probes or bounded experimental changes, but after the true root cause is established, audit the complete attempt set and revert every superseded probe, workaround, fallback, and speculative change. The final retained patch must be the minimal root-cause correction plus focused regression coverage. Explicitly perform this cleanup before reporting the issue fixed.

- Restored continuously advancing MVZ/FLIC playback on modern Windows. Runtime evidence showed frame 2 scheduling `next_frame_time` roughly 105 minutes ahead even though decoding, palette conversion, dirty propagation, and BitBlt worked. Original `RuntimeSoundWindowProcedure` (`0x004021E0`) passes `WOM_DONE`'s `wParam` (`HWAVEOUT`) to the mixer as its timing marker, while `RuntimeWaveOutCallback` (`0x004035C0`) also supplies `timeGetTime()` in `lParam`. Modern opaque `HWAVEOUT` values break the original arithmetic. With `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`, only `WOM_DONE` now passes the timestamp from `lParam`; `WOM_OPEN` and fixes-OFF retain original `wParam` behavior. Production tracing verified LOGO1 advanced through at least frames 1-20 with changing RGB565 surfaces and the following 150-frame animation also advanced. Both startup variants test their exact marker source; CTest passes 3/3. Production tracing was disabled and its process/log removed.
- Corrected two additional original-storage aliases required for visible animation. Original address `0x0047F3C0` is `RuntimeGameHostContext::bits_per_pixel`, not an independent palette-depth global; the split zero value prevented `ConfigureRuntimeResourcePalette` (`0x00427E60`) from installing indexed-to-RGB565 compositors. Original `SetRuntimeResourceState` (`0x00425930`) reads every transition input from `0x0047F86C` (`RuntimeCommandLoopState::accumulated_tree_flags`), not graphics flags at `0x0047F890`; correcting it lets the original immediate transition open the full display clip without a diagnostic override. Runtime evidence confirmed decoded indexed pixels become nonzero RGB565 pixels and reach BitBlt. The temporary forced clip was removed.

- Corrected the black-screen animation-stream root cause. Runtime tracing proved scripts now transition `ENTRY -> AURICV -> ZEST -> AURICP -> GAG -> FRONT`, and each logo image link is parsed and published, but all animation constructors returned null. Original instructions at `0x0042572F` load the second argument to `OpenCdfArchive` from absolute `0x0047F7D8`; relative to the graphics-host base `0x0047EF60`, this is `RuntimeCommandLoopState::async_file_host` at confirmed offset `+0x878`. Source had incorrectly split that address into an independent zero-initialized `runtime_resource_archive_alternate_stream`, causing the archive to open with ordinary Win32 handles while uncompressed animation entries were later treated as `AsyncFileRecord *`. The variable now aliases the exact embedded async-host field. The construction test asserts that a non-null host identity is forwarded to archive opening. Temporary black-screen traces were removed. Win32 Debug build and CTest pass 3/3; the corrected executable was launched for interactive logo-render confirmation. Ghidra already contains the correct `0x0047F7D8` operand; the bridge's dynamic mutation tools were unavailable to this client after reconnection, so no Ghidra data label was changed.

- Added a default-ON modern-Windows tree-lifetime compatibility shim for the initial `CFG -> ENTRY` PRELOAD transition. The original destruction path recursively frees a child selected as its replacement and then reuses that pointer; on the modern Debug heap this published a null/dangling runtime tree. With `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`, an explicitly selected direct child is detached and promoted into the parent's list position before recursive destruction. Fixes-OFF retains the original behavior. Dual-variant tests cover both outcomes.

- Black-screen startup tracing proved that CDF opening, `Start.cfg` loading, CFG-tree creation, and the script executor all succeed. Corrected `ParseImageFlag` (`0x0040E580`): unknown successfully extracted tokens return zero, while only null/parser-token extraction failure returns `0xFFFFFFFF`. Raw original instructions at `0x0040EA04..0x0040EA26` confirm the final comparison produces either `0x04000000` or zero; `0x0040EA27` is the distinct extraction-failure return. This correction allows `e_START /PRELOAD::ENTRY` to parse and activate `ENTRY`. Tests cover unknown flag fallback into a type-4 script string. The subsequent CFG-root replacement and animation archive-stream failures are resolved by the two entries above.

- Completed the requested minimal-change cleanup after interactive confirmation. Removed the last failed-hypothesis residue: Window-mode exceptions in both original `WM_ACTIVATE` auto-minimize branches and their tests. Previously removed items remain absent: `WM_MOUSEACTIVATE` overrides, foreground/active/focus calls and injectable API, high-bit message-gate bypass, and Debug tracing. The only interaction-specific compatibility behavior retained is the required fixed frame/geometry plus visibility-preserving post-`ShowWindow` styles (`0x12CA0000` Window, `0x92000000` Full Screen). Original procedure control flow is restored. Win32 Debug build, both startup variants, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, and tab/trailing-whitespace checks pass.
- Found and corrected the evidence-backed root cause of the inert/click-through framed window. Final native-state diagnostics showed the enabled top-level HWND had `visible=0` and style `0x06CA0000`: compatibility `UpdateApplicationWindowLayout` called `SetWindowLongA(GWL_STYLE, modern_windows_windowed_style)` after `ShowWindow`, replacing the whole style and clearing dynamic `WS_VISIBLE`. DWM left a stale-looking frame, but Windows correctly excluded the invisible HWND hierarchy from hit testing. Compatibility layout transitions now use `modern_windows_windowed_style | WS_VISIBLE` (`0x12CA0000`) and original fullscreen style `0x82000000 | WS_VISIBLE` (`0x92000000`). Removed the superseded explicit foreground/active/focus API, `WM_MOUSEACTIVATE` overrides, high-bit suppression bypass, and all temporary debug traces. Original fixes-OFF behavior is unchanged. Dual-variant tests assert the visibility-preserving styles; Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, and `check-format` pass. Fresh corrected process 29132 was launched for interactive confirmation. Black rendering remains a separate issue if interaction now succeeds.
- Runtime diagnosis of the apparently still-inactive/unmovable window found that the visible instance was stale process 28632, launched before the latest activation rebuild. GAG's recovered single-instance `FindWindowA("FlcAppClassNT", nullptr)` validation caused all later rebuilt launches to exit before creating a replacement, so runtime observations were unknowingly against the old process. The stale diagnostic process was force-terminated and current Debug `gag.exe` was launched as process 28796 for interactive validation. Do not interpret another launch as testing new code while an existing GAG window remains alive.
- Extended the default-ON `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` Window mode to use a movable fixed-size native frame. The compatibility style is exactly `WS_CAPTION | WS_SYSMENU | WS_MINIMIZEBOX | WS_CLIPCHILDREN` (`0x02CA0000`): it has a title bar, system menu, close/minimize controls, and child clipping, but deliberately omits `WS_THICKFRAME` and `WS_MAXIMIZEBOX`, so the game client remains its required framebuffer size. Initial creation uses this style, and Full Screen/Window transitions apply the appropriate style with `SetWindowLongA` followed by `SetWindowPos(..., SWP_FRAMECHANGED)`. Fixes-OFF creation and layout preserve original behavior. Dual-variant tests cover initial style, transition styles, frame refresh, and unchanged geometry. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, tab, and whitespace checks pass. Ghidra was not changed because this is non-original compatibility behavior.
- Completed a broader Ghidra-xref audit of globals inside the single `InitializeGraphicsHost` backing allocation after the second `ntdll` access violation in `DetectRuntimeResourceType` (`0x00424710`). Corrected all remaining confirmed artificial splits in this state group: resource operations and game-DLL load/unload now share embedded critical section `0x0047F828` (`+0x8C8`); resource failure notifications use host window `0x0047EF60`; named locking retains `0x0047F840`/`0x0047F864`; streamed-resource count uses `0x0047F884` (`+0x924`) while scene/resource wait accounting uses distinct `0x0047F868` (`+0x908`); property value and resource presentation owner both alias script clock `0x0047F898` (`+0x938`); and enter/leave state uses existing deferred/current scene slots `0x0047F8B8/0x0047F8BC`. Regression tests now assert the fourth/fifth critical-section identities, host notification window, named-node parent, and distinct resource counters. Win32 Debug build and CTest 3/3 pass; `check-format`, tab, and edited-file whitespace checks pass. Ghidra was not mutated because the original absolute references were already correct.
- Fixed the runtime lock access violation in `AcquireRuntimeLockRecord` (`0x00425F10`) as an allocation-overlay reconstruction error. Raw Ghidra assembly proves the function enters critical section `0x0047F840` and searches the named-node parent stored at `0x0047F864`. Those are respectively `RuntimeCommandLoopState::path_critical_section` (the fifth critical section initialized by `InitializeGraphicsHost`) and `RuntimeCommandLoopState::media_objects_parent_identity` (the identity returned for `"MMediaObjectsList"`). Source had incorrectly modeled both as independent zero-initialized globals. They now alias the exact embedded fields. Regression tests verify both aliases after graphics-host initialization. Win32 Debug builds and CTest 3/3 pass; `check-format` and tab checks pass. Ghidra original behavior was not changed because its addresses and instructions were already correct.
- Added the third `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` compatibility tranche for a practical original Window mode. When application flag `0x80` selects Window, both initial creation and later `View -> Window` transitions now use `AdjustWindowRect(..., menu=TRUE)` around the native framebuffer dimensions, center the resulting outer rectangle on the desktop, and position the capture child at client origin. Full Screen and all fixes-OFF geometry remain byte-for-byte source-equivalent to the recovered desktop-sized behavior. Focused dual-variant tests cover initial 640x480 window creation, centered outer geometry, client/capture coordinates, transition layout, and unchanged fullscreen behavior. Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, `git diff --check`, and tab checks pass. The rebuilt executable was launched for user-visible geometry inspection; it remained responding, though `Get-Process` did not yet report a main-window handle/title. Ghidra was deliberately unchanged because all new branches are non-original compatibility behavior.
- Added the second default-ON `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` compatibility tranche for modern true-color desktops. Original builds still reject depths above 16 bpp. Fixes builds retain the physical depth in `ApplicationState`, select a non-original virtual original-compatible framebuffer at the current desktop dimensions, and suppress legacy 640x480 physical mode switching. A private `ModernWindowsColorMode` constant currently selects RGB565; both RGB565 (`F800/07E0/001F`) and indexed-8 descriptors are implemented and tested for eventual user selection. Presentation remains through the recovered GDI DIB/`BitBlt` path, so Windows converts to the physical desktop format and no asset conversion was added. Ghidra was deliberately unchanged. Both startup variants and CTest 3/3 pass, as do `check-format`, `git diff --check`, and tab checks. The fixes-enabled game was launched on a true-color desktop and remained alive/responding past the former color-depth rejection; the visible process was left running for user inspection.
- Replaced the final two explicit production failure stubs with non-original zlib 1.3.2 adapters. `FREEGAG_ZLIB_DIR` is a configurable CMake cache path defaulting to `third_party/zlib-1.3.2`; `freegag_zlib` builds only the eight core checksum/deflate/inflate sources and is linked privately into `gag` and both startup-test variants. The reader preserves the original `08 00` raw-DEFLATE and `00 00` stored framing plus `-1`/`-2` results; the writer emits `08 00` and level-9 raw DEFLATE within the caller's capacity. Direct malformed/stored/empty/capacity/round-trip tests pass, and both startup variants open `data/orig/Gag01.cdf` through the default production binding and confirm all 1088 index entries are populated. Fresh Win32 Debug build, CTest 3/3, `check-format`, `git diff --check`, and tab checks pass. Ghidra was deliberately unchanged because both adapters implement delegated library boundaries. No known production failure stub or additional third-party library dependency remains.
- Historical production CDF defect, now resolved: `data/orig/Gag01.cdf` is `CDF97a` with compressed index size 0x2DCB and 1088 entries. `InitializeCdfIndex` allocates and zeroes entry storage and populates the `entries[]` pointers; when its production decompressor was a failure stub, the ignored return caused apparent success with zero-filled records. Ghidra and archive bytes confirm `DecompressCdfBuffer` (`0x0040F8D0`) consumes the `00 00` stored or `08 00` raw-DEFLATE prefix rather than a gzip envelope. The default binding is now the tested zlib adapter described above.
- Added default-ON CMake option `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` and passed `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES=1` privately to the production `gag` target when enabled. Its first compatibility branch changes registry loading at `0x0041EDF0` from original `KEY_ALL_ACCESS` to `KEY_QUERY_VALUE`, and settings saving at `0x0041D060` to `KEY_SET_VALUE`; OFF preserves original access exactly.
