# 2026-08-19 - Fixes-only resizable scaled Window mode implemented

- `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` Window mode now uses a sizing frame and maximize button while retaining the original 640x480 framebuffer and game coordinate system. Fixes-off behavior is unchanged.
- Added `ModernWindowsWindowedScaling` with `integer` (default) and continuous `preserve_aspect` modes. The capture child is centered at the calculated viewport on every non-minimized `WM_SIZE`; the black parent owns unused client-area bars.
- `WM_GETMINMAXINFO` derives the minimum framed tracking size from an exact 640x480 client rectangle. Arbitrary larger frame aspect ratios are allowed.
- GDI presentation, dirty clears, repaint expansion, and inverse mouse mapping now use the viewport dimensions in either fullscreen or Window mode. Exact 640x480 presentation retains `BitBlt`; scaled presentation uses the existing `COLORONCOLOR` `StretchBlt` path.
- Fixes-only HKCU persistence now writes `WindowRectangle` as a framed `RECT` and keeps `WindowPosition` for compatibility. Restore rejects undersized/off-monitor rectangles and migrates the legacy point. Saving uses `WINDOWPLACEMENT::rcNormalPosition`, with `GetWindowRect` fallback, so minimized/maximized shutdown does not replace the preferred normal geometry.
- The latest Window rectangle is preserved across fullscreen transitions. Focused tests cover integer thresholds, continuous aspect scaling, minimum tracking size, resize layout, windowed scaled presentation/input, rectangle validation/migration, and normal-rectangle saving.
- Win32 Debug build and CTest 4/4 pass for fixes-on and fixes-off. Interactive resize, maximize, mouse, restart persistence, and fullscreen round-trip verification remains for the user.

# 2026-08-19 - Stray subtitle `END` root cause confirmed

- Extracted `.RUS` subtitle resources terminate with a script section marker such as `[END]`.
- GAG.EXE `DrawRuntimeGenericText` (`0x00411FF0`) has an explicit switch case for byte `'['` that branches directly to cleanup without drawing it or any following bytes. Raw assembly routes the `'['` jump-table entry to `0x0041222F`.
- Source `draw_runtime_generic_text` is missing that `'['` case even though `measure_runtime_generic_text` already stops at `'['`. Consequently the reconstructed renderer can continue past the measured dialogue into `[END]`; brackets may be visually absent/clipped while `END` remains visible.
- This confirmed original-fidelity reconstruction omission is now corrected outside `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`: `draw_runtime_generic_text` handles `'['` by branching to cleanup exactly like GAG.EXE. A focused draw test seeds a visible bracket glyph and proves `A[END]` emits only `A`.
- Win32 Debug build, fixes-on/fixes-off startup tests, resource test, XTET test, formatting, and whitespace checks pass. Interactive verification of the one-time portal subtitle sequence remains.

# 2026-08-19 - Integrated XTET launch path diagnosed

- A temporary two-sided trace established that `/GAME:xtetdll.dll` resolves through `runtime_display_context.resource_directory`, producing `E:\\xtetdll.dll` in the current installation. `LoadLibraryA` failed with error 126, so ordinal 1 never ran and the script correctly continued back into the main game.
- This was a deployment/path issue rather than an XTET initialization or callback failure. The user chose to place the reconstructed DLL at the original dynamically resolved location instead of adding an executable-directory compatibility fallback.
- All temporary GAG/XTET trace code and generated trace logs were removed. Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass.

# 2026-08-19 - XTET Escape result-reporting re-entry fixed

- Pressing Escape entered reconstructed ordinal 2 while it owned `g_mutex`. The Escape handler stopped gameplay and synchronously sent the score through message `0x7ffc` to the same child window; GAG re-entered ordinal 2 with that host-private message, and the second same-thread `std::mutex` acquisition threw `std::system_error`.
- A later crash stack during matched-piece keyboard draining proved this was not specific to message `0x7ffc`: `PeekMessage` can dispatch an arbitrary sent window message and re-enter ordinal 2 while the outer call owns synchronization. Ghidra had already confirmed the original uses a recursive Win32 critical section; reconstructed `std::mutex` was the incorrect substitute.
- DLL state synchronization now uses `std::recursive_mutex`. The initialization flag is atomic and ordinal 2 checks it before locking, matching the original inactive-state gate and preventing cross-thread failure-report deadlock; it rechecks under the lock before dispatch. The earlier message-specific bypass was removed.
- This is a shared reconstruction correction rather than a `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES` change. Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass. The corrected DLL was copied to `E:\\XTETDLL.DLL`; interactive Escape, focus-change, and matched-input verification remain.

# 2026-08-19 - Scaled XTET mouse hit testing corrected

- Original XTET correctly obtains the cursor with `GetCursorPos` plus `ScreenToClient`, but the modern GAG host can size its capture child larger than XTET's fixed 640x480 framebuffer. The reconstructed DLL therefore passed physical viewport coordinates directly to 640x480 control hit testing, selecting the wrong button or no button.
- `find_pressed_button` retains the original cursor-query path and inverse-scales each in-client coordinate only when client and framebuffer extents differ. Exact-size behavior and out-of-client coordinates remain unchanged. Focused tests cover identity, 2x, continuous aspect, bottom/right edges, and outside values.
- The rebuilt DLL was copied to `E:\\XTETDLL.DLL`. Interactive testing must exercise all visible controls and determine whether the reported Exit/frozen-state symptom was entirely the same mis-hit.

# 2026-08-19 - XTET mutex and callback audit completed

- Audited every production C++ mutex and every XTET game-lock call site against message pumping, synchronous host callbacks, worker callbacks, waits, joins, and destruction. The only production domains are the recursive gameplay lock, the worker's private condition-state mutex, and the standalone loader's already-recursive audio lock.
- `GameWorker` releases its private mutex before interval, tick, and failure callbacks; its only `stop()` callers are ordinal-1 initialization before acquiring gameplay state and destruction. It therefore must remain a non-recursive mutex and has no confirmed callback or lock-order defect. The loader audio lock is already recursive because WinMM can re-enter its callbacks.
- Found and fixed one additional confirmed lifecycle hazard: `g_mutex` was declared after `g_game`, so static destruction destroyed the mutex before `GameState` stopped and joined its worker. Declaration order is now mutex first, game state second, guaranteeing the lock remains alive throughout worker shutdown and member teardown during `FreeLibrary`.
- Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass. The audited DLL was copied to `E:\\XTETDLL.DLL`; repeated Exit/unload and focus/runtime testing remains interactive.

# 2026-08-19 - XTET initial facing variants restored

- Original `CreateFallingFigurine` at `0x1001E5E0` initializes orientation to `+1` or `-1`, not `-3` or `-1`. The decisive raw sequence at `0x1001E68A..0x1001E692` is `DEC EAX; CMP EAX,1; SBB AL,AL; AND AL,2; DEC AL`, producing `+1` for odd random values and `-1` for even values.
- Source previously used `-3` for the odd branch. Since orientation `-3` applies only vertical mirroring while `-1` applies horizontal mirroring, both spawn branches retained the same left/right facing. Spawn selection now uses `+1/-1`, matching the original unmirrored/horizontally mirrored pair.
- Focused spawn and sprite-selection expectations were corrected, and the misleading Ghidra constructor comment was replaced and saved. Win32 Debug build and CTest 4/4 pass. The rebuilt DLL was copied to `E:\\XTETDLL.DLL`; interactive facing verification remains.

# 2026-08-19 - Save/Return released-parser crash corrected

- Rapidly selecting Return after Save exposed a write access violation at the common `parser->cursor` tail in `ExecuteScriptCommands`. The command had already released or replaced the current tree/link, leaving the cached parser pointer invalid.
- Raw GAG.EXE control flow confirms three additional paths branch directly to outer-loop address `0x0042317D` and bypass every common-tail parser access: successful root `PEXIT`/`CONTINUE` replacement, nested deactivation with no continuation, and opcode `0x000D0000` session reset. Source had modeled all three as `restart_outer`, which still restored the saved cursor.
- These paths now return `restart_outer_commit_cursor`, whose executor handling performs no parser/link access after dispatch. This is an original-fidelity correction outside `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`. The Ghidra function comment was updated and saved.
- Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass. Interactive rapid Save -> Return verification remains.
- Root-caused and corrected the persisted-comments first-click-no-change issue at the actual fixes-only ownership boundary. `ApplicationState::flags & 0x02000000` is the persisted/UI “Comments On” preference, while `ScriptRuntimeRoot::flags & 1` is live `NOCOMMENT`. Startup initially synchronizes them, but the later `START.CFG` intro sections set live `NOCOMMENT`; `FRONT` does not clear it. Raw initialization proves the parser root pointer and `game_context + 0x9B0` are the same embedded root, and direct, indirect, and bulk-reset audits found no missing original clear. The original 2030 handler at `0x0041D8B1..0x0041D8FA` toggles from that stale live bit, whereas message 1030 draws from the persisted bit. Under `FREEGAG_WINDOWS_FIXES`, 2030 now toggles the persisted preference as the authoritative value and derives the inverse live bit; fixes-off retains the exact original handler. The rejected message-1030 mutation remains removed. Opposing tests reproduce persisted-On plus live-NOCOMMENT and prove first-click Off under fixes versus unchanged original behavior without fixes. Win32 Debug build, CTest 4/4, `check-format`, and whitespace checks pass; interactive restart/toggle verification remains.
# 2026-08-20 - AMD64 portability conversion builds and passes tests

- Removed the remaining application-specific `__fastcall`/`XTET_ABI` annotations and all source `static_assert` architecture gates at the user's explicit request. Win32-owned `WINAPI`/`CALLBACK` conventions remain intact.
- Converted pointer-bearing runtime identities, scene owners, event-record slots, save state, generic-child contexts, CDF alternate streams, DLL handles/procedures, and callback state to native-width storage. Replaced the x86 all-bits pointer sentinel with a native-width sentinel.
- Recovered intentional command-definition/scene-slot overlap as one aliased type, while splitting unrelated globals that only overlapped because of the original x86 image layout. Removed packed alignment from native synchronization/pointer state and aligned the backing command-loop storage to its actual type.
- Corrected fixed COM vtable indices to use the original four-byte slot numbering rather than native pointer size.
- Replaced the media presentation and scene-descriptor DWORD overlays with `RuntimePresentationTarget` and `DisplaySceneDescriptor`. This prevents AMD64 framebuffer pointers and Win32 handles from being read from truncated slots or structure padding.
- Widened pointer-valued tree deactivation/section-dispatch results and `RuntimeTreeLink84::value_0054`, and made display callback metadata and callback identities typed/native-width.
- Root `build/` is configured for x64 Debug. `gag`, XTET, both extractors, and all test targets build; normal CTest and AddressSanitizer CTest both pass 4/4. The aggregate default build can still fail if the user's running `xtet_loader.exe` locks its output, so validation intentionally builds named targets without terminating it.
- Full interactive AMD64 gameplay remains the immediate acceptance test; compile-time and unit coverage cannot prove every recovered x86 data overlay has been exercised.

# 2026-08-20 - AMD64 installation registry lookup corrected

- The AMD64 build reported that Setup must be run because `HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\ZES't Corp.\\GAG` exists only in the 32-bit registry view; the native 64-bit view has no key. The original installer is 32-bit and wrote `Version`, `Path`, and `set` into that redirected view.
- Fixes-enabled registry reads now explicitly use `KEY_WOW64_32KEY`. Fixes-owned HKCU settings and window-placement reads/writes use the same view, preserving preferences written by prior Win32 builds. Fixes-off registry access remains unchanged.
- The AMD64 `gag` target and both startup test variants build. CTest passes 4/4, `check-format` passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors. Interactive launch remains to confirm startup proceeds past installation validation.

# 2026-08-20 - AMD64 initial scene-reset lock crash corrected

- The first post-registry AMD64 run crashed in `RtlEnterCriticalSection` from `acquire_runtime_lock_record`. The named-lock critical section had been zeroed by original fixed-offset scratch aliases that still addressed offsets `0x95c`, `0x11d8`, and `0x1978` inside the now-widened `RuntimeCommandLoopState`.
- Removed the remaining `graphics_host_storage + offset` aliases. The command-loop state is now a native typed object, while the display-reset array, session-reset array, transition palette, graphics instance, and resource-directory buffer have independent typed storage. Initialization, shutdown, tests, and resource-path access reset the corresponding objects explicitly.
- AMD64 `gag` and all named test targets build. CTest passes 4/4, `check-format` passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors. Interactive launch must confirm the initial scene reset now proceeds.

# 2026-08-20 - AMD64 black-screen script-thread identity corrected

- After separating the former image overlay, `initialize_runtime_graphics` still passed `&graphics_host_state` to `ExecuteScriptCommands`. In the original x86 image this was also the command-loop base; in the portable model it is only the public graphics initialization result, so the script thread read unrelated flags and state and left a responsive black window.
- The script thread now receives `&runtime_display_context`. The runtime-bootstrap test captures the actual `CreateThread` parameter and requires it to equal the command-loop state object.
- AMD64 `gag` and all named test targets build. CTest passes 4/4, `check-format` passes, and the diff has no whitespace errors. Interactive startup remains the acceptance check.
